I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` cities and `n-1` roads
2. Currently, the graph might not be connected (there could be multiple connected components)
3. We need to make the graph connected (so every city can reach every other city)
4. Each day, we can close one existing road and build one new road
5. We need to find the minimum number of days and provide a plan

## Key Insights

1. In a connected graph with `n` vertices, we need exactly `n-1` edges
2. We currently have `n-1` edges, but they might not form a connected graph
3. If the graph has `k` connected components, we need `k-1` additional edges to connect them all
4. Therefore, we need `k-1` days to build new roads and close old ones

## Algorithm Steps

1. Build the graph from input
2. Find the connected components using DFS/BFS
3. Count the number of connected components `k`
4. We need `k-1` days to connect all components
5. For each day, we need to:
   - Close a road that connects different components (or any road)
   - Build a new road that connects two components

## Implementation Strategy

1. Use DFS to find connected components
2. For each component, store the set of vertices
3. To connect components, we can:
   - Pick any edge from a component to another component to close
   - Build an edge that connects two different components

Actually, let me reconsider: Since we have exactly `n-1` edges and `n` vertices, the graph can have multiple components. We need to remove one edge from each component to build connections across components.

Wait, no. The key insight is simpler: if we have `k` components, we need exactly `k-1` edges to connect them. We can do this by:
1. Removing one edge from each of the components that need to be connected
2. Adding `k-1` new edges to connect the components

But actually, we can be more efficient: we just need to connect `k` components with `k-1` edges. We can remove any edge from any component (it doesn't matter which) and add an edge between two different components.

## Step-by-Step Solution

```python
def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    if n == 2:
        # Only one possible road, already connected
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Build adjacency list
    adj = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
    edges = []
    
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        adj[a].append(b)
        adj[b].append(a)
        edges.append((a, b))
    
    # Find connected components using DFS
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    def dfs(v, comp):
        visited[v] = True
        comp.append(v)
        for neighbor in adj[v]:
            if not visited[neighbor]:
                dfs(neighbor, comp)
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            comp = []
            dfs(i, comp)
            components.append(comp)
    
    k = len(components)  # number of connected components
    
    # If already connected (k = 1), no days needed
    if k == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # We need k-1 days to connect all components
    t = k - 1
    print(t)
    
    # Plan the days
    # For each day, we need to:
    # 1. Close one edge (from any component)
    # 2. Build a new edge connecting two different components
    
    # We'll build a bridge between components one by one
    # First, create a list of component representatives
    comp_rep = []
    for i, comp in enumerate(components):
        comp_rep.append(comp[0])  # use first vertex as representative
    
    # For each day, we'll connect two components
    # We need to close one edge - we can close any edge that exists
    # We'll close an edge from the first component that has multiple vertices
    # Or we can close any edge (since we just need to remove one)
    
    # Let's go through components and build connections
    for i in range(k - 1):
        # Connect component i and component i+1
        comp1 = components[i]
        comp2 = components[i + 1]
        
        # We need to close one edge - let's pick any edge that exists in the original graph
        # We can close any edge from the entire graph
        # To be safe, let's close an edge within component i (if it exists) or any edge
        
        # If component i has at least 2 vertices, we can close any edge within it
        # But we need to close an edge that doesn't create disconnected components
        # Actually, we can close any existing edge - the algorithm will work
        
        # Let's close an edge from the first component (first vertex to any neighbor)
        # We'll use the first edge in the first component
        if len(comp1) >= 2:
            # Find the first edge in the original graph that connects two vertices in comp1
            # Actually, since we have the original edges, we can look for any edge
            
            # We'll just close any edge from the original list that connects vertices in comp1
            found = False
            for edge in edges:
                a, b = edge
                if a in comp1 and b in comp1:
                    # Close this edge
                    print(f"{a} {b} {comp1[0]} {comp2[0]}")
                    # Remove this edge from our edges list (for next iterations)
                    # But we don't actually need to remove it, as we're just giving the plan
                    found = True
                    break
            
            if not found:
                # Fall back to closing any edge
                # Since we have the graph, we can pick any edge
                # Pick the first edge in the original list
                a, b = edges[0]
                print(f"{a} {b} {comp1[0]} {comp2[0]}")
        else:
            # Component i has only one vertex - no edge within it
            # Pick first edge from any edge
            a, b = edges[0]
            print(f"{a} {b} {comp1[0]} {comp2[0]}")

solve()
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` cities and `n-1` roads
2. Currently, the cities are not fully connected (we need a spanning tree)
3. We need to make the graph connected so that every city can reach every other city
4. Each day, we can close one existing road and build one new road
5. We need to find the minimum number of days and provide the plan

## Key Insight

Since we have `n` nodes and only `n-1` edges, the graph might already be a tree (connected) or it might have cycles and disconnected components.

- If the graph is already connected, we need 0 days
- If the graph has cycles or disconnected components, we need to break cycles or connect components

The key insight is that we need to achieve a spanning tree. The minimum number of days needed is equal to the number of extra edges or disconnected components.

## Algorithm Analysis

1. First, check if the current graph is connected. If it is, answer is 0.
2. If not, we need to add edges to connect components. 
3. The minimum number of edges to add in a forest with `k` components is `k-1`.

But we also need to consider that we can remove edges to break cycles. Since we have exactly `n-1` edges, if the graph is not connected, we have at least one cycle or multiple components.

Actually, let me reconsider: with `n` nodes and `n-1` edges, the graph can have:
- 1 component (connected) + cycles (extra edges in a tree)
- Multiple components (not connected)

If we have multiple components, we need to connect them. Since we can remove one edge and add one edge per day, we need to find the minimum number of operations.

The minimum number of days needed is equal to the number of connected components minus 1. 

However, if there are cycles, we need to break them by removing edges. But since we're adding edges to connect components, we only need to break cycles that prevent connectivity.

- Is a tree (connected), or 
- Has cycles and multiple components

But if it has cycles, removing edges can break cycles, and adding edges can connect components.

The number of days needed is equal to the number of extra edges (cycles) or the number of components - 1, whichever is smaller?

Actually, let's think about it differently:

For a graph with `n` nodes and `n-1` edges:
- If connected: 0 days (already a tree)
- If not connected: it has `k` components where `k > 1`. To connect `k` components, we need `k-1` edges. 

Since we can remove one edge and add one edge per day, we need `k-1` days.

But initially, we have `n-1` edges. If we have `k` components, we need `k-1` edges. So we need to remove cycles and add connections.

The minimum number of days is `k-1`, where `k` is the number of connected components.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Build the graph from input
2. Find connected components using DFS/BFS
3. If number of components is 1, answer is 0
4. If number of components is > 1, answer is (number of components - 1)
5. For the plan: 
   - Remove an edge from a cycle (choose any edge in a cycle)
   - Add an edge to connect two components

## Implementation

```python
def find_components(graph, n):
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            component = []
            stack = [i]
            visited[i] = True
            
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                component.append(node)
                
                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        visited[neighbor] = True
                        stack.append(neighbor)
            
            components.append(component)
    
    return components

def find_cycle_edge(graph, n):
    # Find any edge that is part of a cycle
    # We can pick any edge that connects two nodes already in the same component
    # For a tree, no cycle exists. For a graph with n nodes and n-1 edges, 
    # if it's not a tree, it has cycles.
    
    # Find an edge that connects two nodes in the same component that aren't directly connected
    # Actually, simpler: in a graph with n nodes and n-1 edges, if it's not connected,
    # we can just pick any edge in a component that has more than one connection.
    
    # Check each edge to see if it creates a cycle in a component
    # For each component, if there's a node with degree >= 2, we can remove one of its edges
    
    # Actually, for a spanning tree with extra edges, we can just pick any edge that forms a cycle
    # Let's find a cycle using DFS
    
    parent = [-1] * (n + 1)
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    
    def dfs_find_cycle(start):
        stack = [(start, -1)]
        visited[start] = True
        
        while stack:
            node, par = stack.pop()
            for neighbor in graph[node]:
                if neighbor == par:
                    continue
                if visited[neighbor]:
                    # Found a cycle - parent of neighbor is par, neighbor is node
                    # The edge between par and node forms a cycle with edge between neighbor and node
                    # Actually, edge between node and neighbor forms a cycle with path from node to neighbor
                    # But we just need to find any edge that creates a cycle
                    # Return the edge (node, neighbor) to remove
                    return node, neighbor
                visited[neighbor] = True
                parent[neighbor] = node
                stack.append((neighbor, node))
        
        return None, None
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            # Start DFS from unvisited component
            result = dfs_find_cycle(i)
            if result != (None, None):
                return result
    
    return None, None

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    if n == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    edges = []
    graph = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
    
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a, b))
        graph[a].append(b)
        graph[b].append(a)
    
    # Find connected components
    components = find_components(graph, n)
    num_components = len(components)
    
    if num_components == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # We need to connect num_components components
    # Number of days needed = num_components - 1
    
    t = num_components - 1
    print(t)
    
    # Plan: 
    # 1. Find an edge to remove (any edge that creates a cycle or is in a component)
    # 2. Find an edge to add (to connect two components)
    
    # First, we need to find a specific edge to remove
    # We can find any edge in a cycle, or any edge that is not part of the spanning tree
    
    # Let's find any edge that won't disconnect the graph
    # We can just pick any edge that connects two nodes in the same component
    # And then add an edge to connect two different components
    
    # First, build a set of components for easy lookup
    comp_id = {}
    for idx, comp in enumerate(components):
        for node in comp:
            comp_id[node] = idx
    
    # Find a non-essential edge to remove
    # We can just pick any edge that connects nodes in the same component
    # Since we have extra edges, we can remove one arbitrary edge
    
    # Let's pick the first edge to remove
    # We need to make sure we don't break connectivity
    # Pick any edge where both endpoints are in the same component
    
    # Find such an edge
    edge_to_remove = None
    for a, b in edges:
        if comp_id[a] == comp_id[b]:
            edge_to_remove = (a, b)
            break
    
    # If we couldn't find one, it means we have a tree, which shouldn't happen when num_components > 1
    # So edge_to_remove should exist
    
    # Now, find an edge to add to connect two components
    # Pick two components and connect them
    # Pick the first two components
    comp1 = components[0]
    comp2 = components[1]
    
    # Pick any node from comp1 and any node from comp2
    node1 = comp1[0]
    node2 = comp2[0]
    
    # But we need to make sure edge exists in the original list so we don't add duplicate
    # Actually, we're allowed to add any edge
    edge_to_add = (node1, node2)
    
    # Output the plan
    # For each day, remove one edge and add one edge
    # We need t days, each day removes one edge and adds one edge
    
    # Actually, we can do it all at once: each day we remove an edge and add an edge
    # We need to remove one edge from a cycle (or redundant edge) and add one edge to connect components
    
    # But we can do a sequence: remove one edge, then add one edge to connect two components
    # However, after removing one edge, the graph might still be disconnected, so we need to do multiple steps
    
    # Actually, each day we can remove one edge and add one edge. We need to make all components connected.
    # So we can:
    # Day 1: remove edge e1, add edge (p1, p2) where p1, p2 from different components
    # Day 2: remove another redundant edge, add another connection if needed
    # But if we have multiple components, we only need one edge to connect them all
    
    # The minimum number of days is (number of components - 1)
    
    # So we need (num_components - 1) operations
    
    # We can do:
    # For i in range(t):
    #   remove one redundant edge
    #   add one edge to connect two components
    
    # But we need to ensure the edge to add connects two different components
    
    # Let's find a set of edges that are redundant (in the same component)
    # For each component, find edges within it
    
    # Since we only need (num_components - 1) operations, we can remove one redundant edge per day
    # and add one connecting edge per day, but we only need (num_components - 1) connecting edges
    
    # Actually, we can remove one redundant edge and add one connecting edge in each day
    
    # We need to connect all components. We can do this in (num_components - 1) days.
    
    # Find all redundant edges (edges within the same component)
    redundant_edges = []
    for a, b in edges:
        if comp_id[a] == comp_id[b]:
            redundant_edges.append((a, b))
    
    # We need to remove one redundant edge per day
    # We need to add one connecting edge per day
    
    # But we don't need to add more than (num_components - 1) edges
    # Actually, we can add one connecting edge each day, and that will help connect components
    
    # How to connect components?
    # We have components. To connect them, we need (num_components - 1) edges.
    # We can pick any two components and connect them with one edge.
    
    # Since we're doing multiple days, we can:
    # - In day i: remove a redundant edge, add a connecting edge (to connect two components)
    
    # But the connecting edge should connect two components
    
    # After removing a redundant edge, we might break a connection, so we need to be careful
    
    # However, since we have exactly n-1 edges initially, and we're adding edges, we need to think differently.
    
    # Actually, the problem allows us to remove one edge and add one edge per day.
    # We need to end with exactly n-1 edges and the graph connected.
    
    # Strategy:
    # 1. Remove one redundant edge (any edge within a component)
    # 2. Add an edge that connects two different components
    
    # This will reduce the number of components by 1 (connection) and remove one redundant edge.
    
    # We need to do this (num_components - 1) times.
    
    # So the plan for t days:
    # For each day i from 0 to t-1:
    #   remove one redundant edge
    #   add an edge that connects two components (we can rotate the components)
    
    # But we need to ensure the final graph has only n-1 edges and is connected.
    
    # Let's implement it
    
    # First, get the list of components
    comp_groups = components
    
    # For each day, we'll remove a redundant edge and add a connecting edge
    # We'll add a connecting edge to merge two components
    
    # Find redundant edges
    redundant_edges = []
    for a, b in edges:
        if comp_id[a] == comp_id[b]:
            redundant_edges.append((a, b))
    
    # If no redundant edges, then the graph is a tree and connected - shouldn't happen with multiple components
    # But it's possible if components are not fully connected? No, with n-1 edges and multiple components, 
    # there must be redundant edges or it's impossible.
    
    # Actually, in a forest with n nodes and n-1 edges, if there are k components, 
    # the total number of edges is (n - k). But we have n-1 edges, so n-1 = n-k thus k=1.
    # This is a contradiction!
    
    # Wait! I made a mistake. In a forest (acyclic graph), number of edges = n - k.
    # We have n-1 edges, so n-1 = n - k, therefore k = 1.
    
    # So having multiple components is impossible with n-1 edges unless there's a cycle?
    
    # Actually, the formula is: in any graph, number of edges = n - c + f, where f is number of cycles.
    # More precisely: number of edges = n - c + c_cycles.
    
    # For a forest (no cycles), edges = n - c.
    # With edges = n-1, then n-1 = n - c, so c = 1.
    # So the graph must have exactly one component? No, that can't be.
    
    # Correction: in a forest with c components, edges = n - c.
    # We have edges = n - 1, so n - 1 = n - c, therefore c = 1.
    
    # This means with n nodes and n-1 edges, if there are no cycles, then there is exactly one component.
    
    # Therefore, if we have n-1 edges and the graph is not connected, it must contain cycles.
    
    # So the graph has c components and at least one cycle.
    
    # The number of edges is n-1, which must be at least (n - c) for a forest.
    # So n-1 >= n - c, which gives c >= 1.
    
    # The excess edges over a forest is (n-1) - (n-c) = c-1.
    # So we have c-1 cycles.
    
    # We need to remove c-1 edges to eliminate cycles and make it a forest.
    # Then we still have (n - c) edges, so the graph is disconnected with c components.
    # But we need to connect c components, which requires c-1 edges.
    
    # So total edges to remove and edges to add: (c-1) removals + (c-1) additions = 2(c-1) days?
    
    # But we can do it in c-1 days:
    # Each day: remove one edge from a cycle, add one edge to connect two components.
    # After removing one edge from a cycle, we reduce the number of cycles by 1.
    # After adding an edge between two components, we reduce the number of components by 1.
    
    # We start with c components and (c-1) cycles.
    # We want to reach 1 component.
    
    # Each day: we remove one edge from a cycle and add one edge to connect two components.
    # This reduces components by 1 and reduces cycles by 1.
    
    # So after (c-1) days, we have 1 component and 0 cycles.
    
    # Therefore, the answer is (c - 1) days.
    
    # Plan:
    # For day i in range(c-1):
    #   remove an edge that is part of a cycle
    #   add an edge that connects two components
    
    # We need to find:
    # 1. An edge that is part of a cycle (we can find by checking if removing it preserves connectivity)
    # 2. Two components to connect
    
    # Find a cycle edge: we can find any edge that, when removed, doesn't disconnect the graph
    
    # We'll do:
    # Find any edge (a,b) such that removing it doesn't disconnect any component
    # This edge is in a cycle
    
    # Since the graph has c components and c-1 cycles, there must be many such edges
    
    # For simplicity, let's just use any edge where a and b are in the same component (since components can have cycles)
    
    # But actually, if two nodes are in the same component, and the edge between them exists, it might be redundant.
    
    # We can find a redundant edge by checking if there's another path between a and b
    
    # For now, we'll just pick the first redundant edge we find
    
    # Find redundant edge (within same component)
    redundant_edge = None
    for a, b in edges:
        if comp_id[a] == comp_id[b]:
            redundant_edge = (a, b)
            break
    
    # Find edges to add to connect components
    # We'll connect the first component to the second component in each day
    # But we might need to connect different pairs
    
    # Since we have (c-1) days, we need to connect all components
    # We can connect them in sequence
    
    # For each day, we'll add an edge between two components
    
    # Use the first two components for the first connection
    # Then keep combining
    
    # Actually, we can do:
    # Day 0: remove redundant_edge, add edge between comp1 and comp2
    # Day 1: remove another redundant edge, add edge between new component (comp1+comp2) and comp3
    # And so on
    
    # So we need to find redundant edges and connecting edges
    
    # Create a list of all redundant edges
    redundant_edges_list = []
    for a, b in edges:
        if comp_id[a] == comp_id[b]:
            redundant_edges_list.append((a, b))
    
    # We need c-1 redundant edges removals and c-1 connecting edges
    
    # However, we can just use one redundant edge and one connecting edge per day
    
    # So we'll use the first redundant edge found, and connect components
    
    # But we need to be careful about the final graph having n-1 edges
    
    # Plan:
    # For day in range(t):
    #   remove one redundant edge
    #   add one edge between two different components
    
    # We need to choose which components to connect in each day
    
    # For simplicity, we'll connect the first component to the second, then the new component to the third, etc.
    
    # But we need to ensure the final graph has exactly n-1 edges
    
    # Since we remove 1 edge per day and add 1 edge per day, total edges remain the same.
    
    # The actions:
    # Day i:
    #   Remove edge redundant_edges_list[i % len(redundant_edges_list)]
    #   Add edge between comp1[i] and comp2[i] for connecting
    
    # But we need to ensure the components are different
    
    # Let's prepare the connecting edges
    comp_list = components
    
    # Find all possible connections between different components
    # For each day, we need to add an edge between two different components
    # We'll use the first component with the next, then rotate
    
    # In day i, connect comp_list[i] and comp_list[i+1] (using a specific pair)
    
    # Actually, we can connect comp_list[0] with comp_list[1], then (comp_list[0]+comp_list[1]) with comp_list[2], etc.
    # But we need to pair them
    
    # For simplicity, we'll just add an edge between comp_list[0] and comp_list[1] in day 0,
    # and in subsequent days, we'll connect with different pairs.
    
    # But we only need c-1 edges total.
    
    # We'll create a list of connecting edges
    connect_edges = []
    for i in range(t):  # t = c-1
        # Connect first component to the (i+1)th component
        if i < len(comp_list) - 1:
            connect_edges.append((comp_list[0][0], comp_list[i+1][0]))
        else:
            # Connect with the last component
            connect_edges.append((comp_list[0][0], comp_list[-1][0]))
    
    # But this might not be efficient
    
    # Simpler: just connect comp_list[0] with comp_list[1], comp_list[0] with comp_list[2], etc.
    # But we can just use one sequence
    
    # Let's just use connect_edges = [(comp_list[0][0], comp_list[1][0]), (comp_list[0][0], comp_list[2][0]), ...]
    
    # Actually, we can do in any order
    
    # Since we only need (c-1) days, we can just use the first c-1 pairs of components
    
    connect_edges = []
    for i in range(1, len(comp_list)):
        connect_edges.append((comp_list[0][0], comp_list[i][0]))
    
    # Now, we have t days
    # For each day i:
    #   remove redundant_edges_list[i % len(redundant_edges_list)]
    #   add connect_edges[i]
    
    # But we need to ensure we have enough redundant edges
    
    # Since we have at least (c-1) redundant edges (because c-1 > 0), we're good
    
    # However, if there are fewer redundant edges than (c-1), then our logic is wrong
    
    # But the number of redundant edges is (number of edges in the forest) = (n-1) - (n-c) = c-1
    # So there are exactly c-1 redundant edges.
    
    # So len(redundant_edges_list) should be c-1
    
    # Therefore, we can use it.
    
    # Output for each day
    for i in range(t):
        if i < len(redundant_edges_list):
            a, b = redundant_edges_list[i]
            u, v = connect_edges[i]
        else:
            # This shouldn't happen
            a, b = redundant_edges_list[0]
            u, v = connect_edges[0]
        
        print(f"{a} {b} {u} {v}")

solve()
```

Wait, I see an issue with my component ID handling. Let me fix it and make it cleaner.

```python
def find_components(graph, n):
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            component = []
            stack = [i]
            visited[i] = True
            
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                component.append(node)
                
                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        visited[neighbor] = True
                        stack.append(neighbor)
            
            components.append(component)
    
    return components

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    edges = []
    graph = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
    
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a, b))
        graph[a].append(b)
        graph[b].append(a)
    
    # Find connected components
    components = find_components(graph, n)
    num_components = len(components)
    
    # If already connected, no days needed
    if num_components == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Number of days needed is (number of components - 1)
    t = num_components - 1
    print(t)
    
    # Find redundant edges (edges within the same component)
    # We'll assign a component id to each node
    comp_id = {}
    for idx, comp in enumerate(components):
        for node in comp:
            comp_id[node] = idx
    
    # Find all redundant edges (edges connecting two nodes in the same component)
    redundant_edges = []
    for a, b in edges:
        if comp_id[a] == comp_id[b]:
            redundant_edges.append((a, b))
    
    # Find connecting edges (between different components)
    # We'll connect components sequentially
    connect_edges = []
    for i in range(1, len(components)):
        # Connect the first component to each subsequent component
        connect_edges.append((components[0][0], components[i][0]))
    
    # Output the plan: for each day, remove one redundant edge and add one connecting edge
    for i in range(t):
        a, b = redundant_edges[i]
        u, v = connect_edges[i]
        print(f"{a} {b} {u} {v}")
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Berland Government decided to improve relations with neighboring countries. First of all, it was decided to build new roads so that from each city of Berland and neighboring countries it became possible to reach all the others. There are n cities in Berland and neighboring countries in total and exactly n - 1 two-way roads. Because of the recent financial crisis, the Berland Government is strongly pressed for money, so to build a new road it has to close some of the existing ones. Every day it is possible to close one existing road and immediately build a new one. Your task is to determine how many days would be needed to rebuild roads so that from each city it became possible to reach all the others, and to draw a plan of closure of old roads and building of new ones.

Input

The first line contains integer n (2 ≤ n ≤ 1000) — amount of cities in Berland and neighboring countries. Next n - 1 lines contain the description of roads. Each road is described by two space-separated integers ai, bi (1 ≤ ai, bi ≤ n, ai ≠ bi) — pair of cities, which the road connects. It can't be more than one road between a pair of cities. No road connects the city with itself.

Output

Output the answer, number t — what is the least amount of days needed to rebuild roads so that from each city it became possible to reach all the others. Then output t lines — the plan of closure of old roads and building of new ones. Each line should describe one day in the format i j u v — it means that road between cities i and j became closed and a new road between cities u and v is built. Cities are numbered from 1. If the answer is not unique, output any.

Examples

Input

2
1 2


Output

0


Input

7
1 2
2 3
3 1
4 5
5 6
6 7


Output

1
3 1 3 7

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` cities connected by `n-1` roads
2. Currently, the graph might not be connected (it could have multiple connected components)
3. We need to make the graph connected so that every city can reach every other city
4. Each day, we can close one existing road and build one new road
5. We need to find the minimum number of days and provide the plan

## Key Insights

1. A connected graph with `n` vertices needs at least `n-1` edges
2. We currently have exactly `n-1` edges
3. If the graph is already connected, we need 0 days
4. If the graph is not connected, we have multiple connected components
5. To connect `k` components, we need `k-1` edges
6. Since we can only build one edge per day, we need exactly `(number of components - 1)` days

## Algorithm Steps

1. Build the graph from input
2. Find connected components using DFS/BFS
3. Count the number of components
4. If components = 1, output 0 (already connected)
5. If components > 1, we need (components - 1) days
6. For each day, connect two different components by removing an edge from one component and adding a new edge between components

## Detailed Plan

1. Find all connected components
2. For each component, store the list of cities in it
3. For each day, take one edge from any component and connect two different components
4. To avoid creating cycles, we should remove an edge that doesn't create a cycle in the component we're removing from

The key insight is that since we currently have exactly `n-1` edges for `n` vertices, if the graph is not connected, it must have at least 2 components. We need to reduce the number of components by 1 each day.

## Implementation

```python
def find_components(graph, n):
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            component = []
            stack = [i]
            visited[i] = True
            
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                component.append(node)
                
                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        visited[neighbor] = True
                        stack.append(neighbor)
            
            components.append(component)
    
    return components

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    if n == 2:
        # Special case for 2 nodes
        roads = []
        for _ in range(n - 1):
            a, b = map(int, input().split())
            roads.append((a, b))
        
        # Check if already connected - with 2 nodes and 1 edge, it's always connected
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Read the roads
    roads = []
    edges = {}
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        roads.append((a, b))
        edges[a] = edges.get(a, []) + [b]
        edges[b] = edges.get(b, []) + [a]
    
    # Find connected components
    components = find_components(edges, n)
    
    # If already connected (only one component), no work needed
    if len(components) == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Number of days needed is (number of components - 1)
    days = len(components) - 1
    print(days)
    
    # Plan the operations
    # We need to connect components one by one
    # For each day, we remove one edge from a component and add an edge between two components
    
    # For simplicity, we'll take the first edge from the first component and use it to connect
    # two components. We remove an edge from the component and add an edge between components.
    
    # We'll pick the first component's first edge to remove
    # Connect the first component with the second component, etc.
    
    # Create a list of all components
    comp_list = components[:]
    
    # For each day, connect two components
    for day in range(days):
        # Connect component[day] with component[day+1]
        # First, remove an edge from any component (we'll remove from the first component)
        # Actually, we need to remove an edge from a component that is not the current one
        
        # For simplicity: remove an arbitrary edge from the first component
        # and add an edge between components[day] and components[day+1]
        
        # We'll remove the first edge from the first component and add edge between components[day] and components[day+1]
        
        # But we need to make sure the chosen edge actually exists
        
        # Let's pick the first edge from any component that has more than one node
        # If components[day] has more than one node, we can remove one of its edges
        # If not, we need to be careful
        
        # Actually, since we have a graph, we can just pick any existing edge
        
        # Let's just take the first edge in the graph and remove it if it's from a component we're connecting
        # But we need to be systematic
        
        # For simplicity: remove an edge from the first component that connects to any node in the first component
        if day == 0:
            # Remove an edge from the first component
            # Find any edge in the first component
            comp0 = components[0]
            comp0_edges = []
            for node in comp0:
                for neighbor in edges[node]:
                    if neighbor in comp0:
                        comp0_edges.append((node, neighbor))
            
            if comp0_edges:
                # Remove the first edge
                node1, node2 = comp0_edges[0]
                # Add edge between components[0] and components[1]
                comp1 = components[1]
                node_from_comp1 = comp1[0]  # pick first node from second component
                node_to_comp1 = comp0[0]    # pick first node from first component (should be in first component)
                # We need to add edge between one node from comp0 and one from comp1
                # Pick first node from comp0 and first node from comp1
                u = comp0[0]
                v = comp1[0]
                
                print(f"{node1} {node2} {u} {v}")
            else:
                # This shouldn't happen in a connected component
                print(f"1 2 {components[0][0]} {components[1][0]}")
                
        else:
            # For subsequent days, connect components[day] and components[day+1]
            comp_day = components[day]
            comp_day_plus = components[day + 1]
            
            # Find any edge in comp_day to remove
            comp_day_edges = []
            for node in comp_day:
                for neighbor in edges[node]:
                    if neighbor in comp_day:
                        comp_day_edges.append((node, neighbor))
            
            if comp_day_edges:
                node1, node2 = comp_day_edges[0]
            else:
                # Pick any two nodes from comp_day
                node1, node2 = comp_day[0], comp_day[1]
            
            # Add edge between comp_day and comp_day_plus
            u = comp_day[0]
            v = comp_day_plus[0]
            
            print(f"{node1} {node2} {u} {v}")
```

```python
def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    # Read the roads
    roads = []
    edges = {}
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        roads.append((a, b))
        edges[a] = edges.get(a, []) + [b]
        edges[b] = edges.get(b, []) + [a]
    
    # Find connected components using DFS
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            component = []
            stack = [i]
            visited[i] = True
            
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                component.append(node)
                
                for neighbor in edges.get(node, []):
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        visited[neighbor] = True
                        stack.append(neighbor)
            
            components.append(component)
    
    # If already connected, no need for changes
    if len(components) == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Number of days needed to connect all components
    days = len(components) - 1
    print(days)
    
    # For each day, we need to:
    # 1. Remove one edge from any component (specifically from a component that has multiple nodes)
    # 2. Add a new edge connecting two different components
    
    # We'll process components in order
    for day in range(days):
        # Connect components[day] and components[day+1]
        
        # Find an edge to remove from components[day]
        # We need to find any edge within components[day]
        edge_to_remove = None
        
        for node in components[day]:
            for neighbor in edges.get(node, []):
                if neighbor in components[day]:
                    edge_to_remove = (node, neighbor)
                    break
            if edge_to_remove:
                break
        
        # If no internal edge found (shouldn't happen for components with size > 1)
        if edge_to_remove is None:
            # Pick any two nodes from components[day]
            u, v = components[day][0], components[day][1]
            edge_to_remove = (u, v)
        
        # Add edge between components[day] and components[day+1]
        # Connect first node of components[day] with first node of components[day+1]
        u_new = components[day][0]
        v_new = components[day + 1][0]
        
        print(f"{edge_to_remove[0]} {edge_to_remove[1]} {u_new} {v_new}")
```

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    # Read the roads
    roads = []
    edges = {}
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        roads.append((a, b))
        edges[a] = edges.get(a, []) + [b]
        edges[b] = edges.get(b, []) + [a]
    
    # Find connected components using DFS
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            component = []
            stack = [i]
            visited[i] = True
            
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                component.append(node)
                
                for neighbor in edges.get(node, []):
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        visited[neighbor] = True
                        stack.append(neighbor)
            
            components.append(component)
    
    # If already connected, no need for changes
    if len(components) == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Number of days needed to connect all components
    days = len(components) - 1
    print(days)
    
    # For each day, we need to:
    # 1. Remove one edge from any component (specifically from a component that has multiple nodes)
    # 2. Add a new edge connecting two different components
    
    # We'll process components in order
    for day in range(days):
        # Connect components[day] and components[day+1]
        
        # Find an edge to remove from components[day]
        # We need to find any edge within components[day]
        edge_to_remove = None
        
        for node in components[day]:
            for neighbor in edges.get(node, []):
                if neighbor in components[day]:
                    edge_to_remove = (node, neighbor)
                    break
            if edge_to_remove:
                break
        
        # If no internal edge found (shouldn't happen for components with size > 1)
        if edge_to_remove is None:
            # Pick any two nodes from components[day]
            u, v = components[day][0], components[day][1]
            edge_to_remove = (u, v)
        
        # Add edge between components[day] and components[day+1]
        # Connect first node of components[day] with first node of components[day+1]
        u_new = components[day][0]
        v_new = components[day + 1][0]
        
        print(f"{edge_to_remove[0]} {edge_to_remove[1]} {u_new} {v_new}")

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` cities connected by `n-1` roads
2. We need to make the graph connected so that every city can reach every other city
3. Each day we can close one existing road and build one new road
4. We need to find the minimum number of days required

## Key Insights

1. A connected graph with `n` nodes needs exactly `n-1` edges
2. We currently have exactly `n-1` edges, but they might form multiple connected components
3. If the graph is already connected, we need 0 days
4. If the graph has `k` connected components, we need `k-1` days to connect them
5. To connect `k` components, we need `k-1` edges

## Algorithm Steps

1. Build the graph from the input
2. Find connected components using DFS/BFS
3. Count the number of connected components
4. If components = 1, answer is 0
5. If components > 1, answer is components - 1
6. For the construction plan, pick one vertex from each component (except the last one) and connect them

## Implementation Strategy

1. Use union-find or DFS to find connected components
2. For each component, pick one representative node
3. Connect all but the last component to the last component

```python
from collections import defaultdict, deque

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    if n == 2:
        # Only one road, already connected
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Read the roads
    edges = []
    graph = defaultdict(list)
    
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a, b))
        graph[a].append(b)
        graph[b].append(a)
    
    # Find connected components using BFS
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            # BFS to find all nodes in this component
            component = []
            queue = deque([i])
            visited[i] = True
            
            while queue:
                node = queue.popleft()
                component.append(node)
                
                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        visited[neighbor] = True
                        queue.append(neighbor)
            
            components.append(component)
    
    # If already connected, no changes needed
    if len(components) == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Number of days needed is (number of components - 1)
    days = len(components) - 1
    
    print(days)
    
    # Create the plan of road modifications
    # We'll connect each component to the last component
    # For each component except the last one, we'll connect a node from it to a node in the last component
    
    # Get the representative nodes from each component
    component_representatives = []
    for comp in components:
        component_representatives.append(comp[0])  # First node as representative
    
    # For each component except the last one, connect it to the last component
    for i in range(len(components) - 1):
        # Take any node from component i and connect to last component's representative
        comp_nodes = components[i]
        last_comp_repres = component_representatives[-1]
        
        # Pick the first node from this component
        pick_node = comp_nodes[0]
        
        # We need to close a road between any two nodes in the original graph
        # That road should be in the same component, and we can close it to make space
        
        # The simplest approach: we can close any edge in the component
        # Actually, we can close any edge in the current graph that connects nodes within the component
        # Let's pick the first edge in the component (we'll find one)
        
        # Find an edge within component i
        edge_to_remove = None
        for node in comp_nodes:
            for neighbor in graph[node]:
                if neighbor in comp_nodes and node < neighbor:
                    edge_to_remove = (node, neighbor)
                    break
            if edge_to_remove:
                break
        
        # If we didn't find an edge, there's only one node in the component - shouldn't happen
        if edge_to_remove is None:
            # Fallback: pick the first edge we know exists
            # For a component with at least 2 nodes, we should have at least one edge
            # Pick any edge from the component
            edge_to_remove = (comp_nodes[0], comp_nodes[1])
        
        # Add the operation: close edge (edge_to_remove[0], edge_to_remove[1])
        # and build edge (pick_node, last_comp_repres)
        print(f"{edge_to_remove[0]} {edge_to_remove[1]} {pick_node} {last_comp_repres}")

solve()
```

Berland Government decided to improve relations with neighboring countries. No road connects the city with itself.

Then output t lines — the plan of closure of old roads and building of new ones. Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` cities connected by `n-1` roads
2. We need to make the graph connected (so every city can reach every other city)
3. Each day, we can close one existing road and build one new road
4. We need to find the minimum number of days and provide a plan

## Key Insights

1. A connected graph with `n` vertices needs at least `n-1` edges
2. We currently have exactly `n-1` edges, so we need to check if the graph is already connected
3. If the graph is already connected, we need 0 days
4. If the graph is not connected, we have multiple connected components
5. To connect `k` components, we need exactly `k-1` new edges (since we can connect any two components with one edge)
6. We can close a road in one component and build a new road to connect two components

## Algorithm

1. Build the graph from input
2. Find connected components using DFS/BFS
3. If there's only 1 component, answer is 0
4. If there are `k` components, we need `k-1` days
5. For each day, we close a road in one component and build a new road between two different components

## Step-by-step solution

Let me trace through the examples:

Example 1: n=2, roads: (1,2)
- Only 1 component → already connected → 0 days

Example 2: n=7, roads: (1,2), (2,3), (3,1), (4,5), (5,6), (6,7)
- Components: {1,2,3} and {4,5,6,7} → 2 components
- Need 1 day to connect them
- We can close any road in one component (e.g., road between 3 and 1)
- Build new road between a city in first component and a city in second component (e.g., 3 and 7)

```python
from collections import defaultdict, deque

def find_components(n, edges):
    # Build adjacency list
    graph = defaultdict(set)
    for a, b in edges:
        graph[a].add(b)
        graph[b].add(a)
    
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            component = []
            queue = deque([i])
            visited[i] = True
            
            while queue:
                node = queue.popleft()
                component.append(node)
                
                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        visited[neighbor] = True
                        queue.append(neighbor)
            
            components.append(component)
    
    return components

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    if n == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    edges = []
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a, b))
    
    # Find connected components
    components = find_components(n, edges)
    
    # If only one component, graph is already connected
    if len(components) == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Number of days needed is (number of components - 1)
    days = len(components) - 1
    
    print(days)
    
    # For each day, we need to close a road and build a new road
    # First, build the graph structure
    graph = defaultdict(set)
    for a, b in edges:
        graph[a].add(b)
        graph[b].add(a)
    
    # We need to create connections between components
    # For each component, we'll find a representative city
    component_representatives = []
    for comp in components:
        # Pick the first city as representative
        component_representatives.append(comp[0])
    
    # For each day, we need to close a road and build a new road
    # We'll close a road in one component and build a road between two components
    for day in range(days):
        # Choose two different components to connect
        comp1_idx = day
        comp2_idx = day + 1 if day + 1 < len(components) else 0
        
        # Get representatives
        rep1 = component_representatives[comp1_idx]
        rep2 = component_representatives[comp2_idx]
        
        # Find a road to close in component comp1
        # We need to find any edge in component comp1
        # Look for any edge between two nodes in comp1
        for a in components[comp1_idx]:
            for b in graph[a]:
                if b in components[comp1_idx] and a != b:
                    # Found a road in component comp1
                    # We close this road (a, b)
                    # We build a new road between rep1 and rep2
                    print(f"{a} {b} {rep1} {rep2}")
                    break
            else:
                continue
            break
        else:
            # This shouldn't happen as each component has at least 2 nodes
            # Fallback - this won't be reached
            for a in components[comp1_idx]:
                for b in graph[a]:
                    if b in components[comp1_idx] and a != b:
                        print(f"{a} {b} {rep1} {rep2}")
                        break
                else:
                    continue
                break

solve()
```

from collections import defaultdict, deque

def find_components(n, edges):
    # Build adjacency list
    graph = defaultdict(set)
    for a, b in edges:
        graph[a].add(b)
        graph[b].add(a)
    
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            component = []
            queue = deque([i])
            visited[i] = True
            
            while queue:
                node = queue.popleft()
                component.append(node)
                
                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        visited[neighbor] = True
                        queue.append(neighbor)
            
            components.append(component)
    
    return components

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    if n == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    edges = []
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a, b))
    
    # Find connected components
    components = find_components(n, edges)
    
    # If only one component, graph is already connected
    if len(components) == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Number of days needed is (number of components - 1)
    days = len(components) - 1
    
    print(days)
    
    # For each day, we need to close a road and build a new road
    # First, build the graph structure
    graph = defaultdict(set)
    for a, b in edges:
        graph[a].add(b)
        graph[b].add(a)
    
    # We need to create connections between components
    # For each component, we'll find a representative city
    component_representatives = []
    for comp in components:
        # Pick the first city as representative
        component_representatives.append(comp[0])
    
    # For each day, we need to close a road and build a new road
    # We'll close a road in one component and build a road between two components
    for day in range(days):
        # Choose two different components to connect
        comp1_idx = day
        comp2_idx = day + 1 if day + 1 < len(components) else 0
        
        # Get representatives
        rep1 = component_representatives[comp1_idx]
        rep2 = component_representatives[comp2_idx]
        
        # Find a road to close in component comp1
        # We need to find any edge in component comp1
        # Look for any edge between two nodes in comp1
        for a in components[comp1_idx]:
            for b in graph[a]:

                    # Found a road in component comp1
                    # We close this road (a, b)
                    # We build a new road between rep1 and rep2
                    print(f"{a} {b} {rep1} {rep2}")
                    break
            else:
                continue
            break
        else:
            # This shouldn't happen as each component has at least 2 nodes
            # Fallback - this won't be reached
            for a in components[comp1_idx]:
                for b in graph[a]:
                    if b in components[comp1_idx] and a != b:
                        print(f"{a} {b} {rep1} {rep2}")
                        break
                else:
                    continue
                break

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` cities connected by `n-1` roads
2. Currently, the graph might not be connected (it could have multiple connected components)
3. We need to make the graph connected (so every city can reach every other city)
4. Each day we can close one existing road and build one new road
5. We need to find the minimum number of days and provide the plan

## Key Insights

1. A tree with `n` nodes has exactly `n-1` edges and is connected
2. If we have `c` connected components, we need `c-1` edges to connect them all
3. We need to find where to remove existing edges and where to add new edges to connect all components

## Strategy

1. First, identify all connected components using DFS/BFS
2. Count the number of components `c`
3. We need `c-1` days to connect all components
4. For each component, we can choose a representative city
5. We need to remove one edge from each of the `c-1` components (we don't need to remove edges from the final tree)
6. Actually, we need to find `c-1` edges to remove and add `c-1` new edges to connect components

Wait, let me reconsider. 

Actually, we have a graph with `n-1` edges. For it to be connected, we need exactly `n-1` edges. If it's not connected, it must have more than one component. The minimum number of edges needed to make a graph with `n` nodes connected is `n-1`, so if we have fewer than `n-1` edges, we can't connect it.

But in this case, we have exactly `n-1` edges. A graph with `n` nodes and `n-1` edges can only be connected if it's a tree.

So if it's not connected, it must contain cycles. The number of connected components `c` will satisfy: number of edges = `n - c + k` where `k` is the number of cycles. 

Actually, for a graph with `n` nodes and `e` edges, if it's not connected, we have `c` components and the edges are distributed among these components. Each component with `v_i` nodes has at most `v_i - 1` edges to be connected.

So if we have `c` components with `v_1, v_2, ..., v_c` nodes, the total edges is at most `(v_1 - 1) + (v_2 - 1) + ... + (v_c - 1) = n - c`.

Since we have `n - 1` edges, we have: `n - 1 ≥ n - c`, which gives `c ≥ 1`. 

Actually, we have exactly `n-1` edges, so the number of edges is `n-1`. For a forest (acyclic graph) with `c` components, we have exactly `n - c` edges. So if we have `n-1` edges, we get `n-1 = n - c`, so `c = 1`.

This suggests the graph must be connected? 

Wait, I think I made a mistake. If we have `n-1` edges and the graph is not connected, then we have at least one cycle. 

The key insight is that if we have `c` connected components, and the graph has `n-1` edges, then we have at least one cycle. The number of edges minus the number of components gives the number of cycles: `(n-1) - (n-c) = c-1`.

So we have `c-1` cycles. To make the graph connected, we need to remove one edge from each cycle except one, and add edges to connect the components.

Actually, we need to remove edges to eliminate cycles and then add edges to connect components.

## Correct Strategy

1. Find all connected components
2. Count components `c`
3. We need `c-1` edges to connect these components
4. We need to eliminate cycles to make the graph a forest, then connect components
5. For each cycle, we can remove one edge to break it
6. We need to remove edges to break all cycles, which gives us `number of cycles = total_edges - (n - c) = (n-1) - (n-c) = c-1`

So we need exactly `c-1` operations.

Actually, since we have `c-1` cycles, we need to remove `c-1` edges (one from each cycle), and then add `c-1` edges to connect the components.

Each operation: close one existing road and build one new road.

So total operations = `c-1`.

## Algorithm

1. Build the graph from input
2. Find connected components using DFS/BFS
3. For each component, choose one representative city
4. For each cycle (we have `c-1` cycles), remove one edge from it
5. Add an edge between representatives of different components to connect them

```python
from collections import defaultdict, deque

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    
    # Build adjacency list
    graph = defaultdict(list)
    edges = []
    
    for _ in range(n - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        graph[a].append(b)
        graph[b].append(a)
        edges.append((a, b))
    
    # Find connected components
    visited = [False] * (n + 1)
    components = []
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            component = []
            queue = deque([i])
            visited[i] = True
            
            while queue:
                node = queue.popleft()
                component.append(node)
                
                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        visited[neighbor] = True
                        queue.append(neighbor)
            
            components.append(component)
    
    num_components = len(components)
    
    # If already connected, no operations needed
    if num_components == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    
    # We need num_components - 1 operations
    operations = num_components - 1
    print(operations)
    
    # For each component, we'll choose a representative (first node)
    representatives = [comp[0] for comp in components]
    
    # We need to connect components
    # We'll remove one edge from a cycle and add an edge between components
    
    # To find an edge to remove, we need to find a cycle
    # Instead, let's find an edge that can be safely removed to break a cycle
    
    # Build a dictionary to track which components each edge is in
    edge_to_component = {}
    
    for a, b in edges:
        # Find which component a and b belong to
        comp_a = None
        comp_b = None
        for idx, comp in enumerate(components):
            if a in comp:
                comp_a = idx
            if b in comp:
                comp_b = idx
        
        # If a and b are in different components, this edge is not part of a cycle
        # But we have n-1 edges with c components, so we have c-1 cycles
        # We can safely remove any edge that is part of a cycle, i.e., where comp_a == comp_b
        
        edge_to_component[(a, b)] = (comp_a, comp_b)
    
    # We need to remove edges that are within the same component
    # We'll remove edges that form cycles
    
    # Since we have c components and n-1 edges, and a forest on c components has n-c edges,
    # we have (n-1) - (n-c) = c-1 cycles
    
    # We'll remove one edge from each cycle, and add connections between components
    
    # For simplicity, let's just find any edge that is within a component
    # Find edges that connect nodes in the same component (cycle edges)
    
    removals = []
    additions = []
    
    # Create a list of edges that form cycles
    cycle_edges = []
    
    for a, b in edges:
        comp_a = -1
        comp_b = -1
        for idx, comp in enumerate(components):
            if a in comp:
                comp_a = idx
            if b in comp:
                comp_b = idx
        
        # If a and b are in the same component, this edge is part of a cycle
        if comp_a == comp_b:
            cycle_edges.append((a, b))
    
    # We have cycle_edges, we need to remove exactly len(cycle_edges) edges?
    # Actually, we have c-1 cycles, so we need to remove c-1 edges
    
    # Remove c-1 edges from cycles
    for i in range(operations):
        # Find any cycle edge to remove
        if cycle_edges:
            a, b = cycle_edges.pop(0)
            removals.append((a, b))
            
            # Add an edge to connect two components
            # Connect component i to component j
            # We'll connect the representative of component i to representative of component (i+1) mod c
            # But we need to add edges between components
            
            # Add an edge between representatives of two different components
            rep_i = representatives[i % num_components]
            rep_j = representatives[(i % num_components + 1) % num_components]
            additions.append((rep_i, rep_j))
    
    # Output the operations
    for a, b in removals:
        for u, v in additions:
            print(a, b, u, v)
            break

solve()
```

Wait, I think there's an issue with my logic. Let me reconsider.

If we have `c` components, we have `c-1` cycles (since total edges = n-1, and a forest with c components has n-c edges). We need to remove one edge from each cycle (c-1 removals), and then add c-1 edges to connect the components.

But I'm not correctly identifying which edges are in cycles.

Let me fix it:

```python
from collections import defaultdict, deque

n = int(input())

# Build adjacency list
graph = defaultdict(list)
edges = []

for _ in range(n - 1):
    a, b = map(int, input().split())
    graph[a].append(b)
    graph[b].append(a)
    edges.append((a, b))

# Find connected components
visited = [False] * (n + 1)
components = []

for i in range(1, n + 1):
    if not visited[i]:
        component = []
        queue = deque([i])
        visited[i] = True
        
        while queue:
            node = queue.popleft()
            component.append(node)
            
            for neighbor in graph[node]:
                if not visited[neighbor]:
                    visited[neighbor] = True
                    queue.append(neighbor)
        
        components.append(component)

num_components = len(components)

# If already connected, no operations needed
if num_components == 1:
    print(0)
else:
    # We need num_components - 1 operations
    operations = num_components - 1
    print(operations)
    
    # For each component, choose a representative
    representatives = [comp[0] for comp in components]
    
    # We need to remove edges that create cycles
    # Find edges that are within the same component
    cycle_edges = []
    
    for a, b in edges:
        comp_a = -1
        comp_b = -1
        for idx, comp in enumerate(components):
            if a in comp:
                comp_a = idx
            if b in comp:
                comp_b = idx
        
        # If a and b are in the same component and are connected, this edge is part of a cycle
        # Actually, if they're in the same component, there's a path between them
        # But in the current graph, if two nodes are in the same component and connected by an edge, 
        # it could be part of a cycle
        if comp_a == comp_b:
            cycle_edges.append((a, b))
    
    # We need to remove exactly (num_components - 1) edges from cycles
    # And add (num_components - 1) edges to connect components
    
    # We'll remove one edge from each cycle
    removals = []
    additions = []
    
    for i in range(operations):
        # Remove any cycle edge
        if cycle_edges:
            a, b = cycle_edges.pop(0)
            removals.append((a, b))
        
        # Add an edge to connect two components
        # Connect representative of component i to representative of component (i+1)
        rep_i = representatives[i % num_components]
        rep_j = representatives[(i % num_components + 1) % num_components]
        additions.append((rep_i, rep_j))
    
    # Output each operation: close edge (a,b), build edge (u,v)
    for a, b in removals:
        for u, v in additions:
            print(a, b, u, v)
            break
```
